--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I'll analyze the aggregation mission and design finite state machines to minimize the score.

**Mission Analysis:**
- Score increases by 1 for every robot OUTSIDE the target region (BLUE-walled strip) each step
- Target region has BLACK floor (both strips have black floor) and BLUE walls
- Need robots to find and stay in the BLUE-walled black strip
- Key perceptions: floor color (BLACK = strip, GRAY = middle), wall/object colors (BLUE = target walls, GREEN = other strip walls)

**Strategy:**
- Robots need to move toward BLUE walls and stay there
- When on BLACK floor near BLUE walls → stay/stop
- When on GRAY floor → move toward BLUE color
- When seeing GREEN → move away from it
- GoToColor with blue (color 6=cyan? Let me check: clr options are 1-6, and wall colors are BLUE and GREEN)

Color mapping: 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan. BLUE walls → clr=3, GREEN walls → clr=2.

**FSM Design:**
- State 0: Explore (find blue walls) → transition to GoToColor(blue) when seeing blue, or when on black floor stop
- State 1: GoToColor blue → when on black floor, stop
- State 2: Stop when on black floor

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I need to see the results from Round 2 to know which machines performed best. Since no scores were provided, I'll reason from first principles and diversify based on my Round 2 designs.

**Key insight re-check:**
- Both strips have BLACK floors; the middle is GRAY
- Target = BLUE-walled strip; other strip = GREEN-walled
- Robots must go to BLACK floor AND be near BLUE walls
- GoToColor(blue=3) should navigate toward blue walls
- GoAwayColor(green=2) pushes away from wrong strip
- On BLACK floor near BLUE walls = stay still

**Refined strategy:**
- The core loop: Explore/move → see BLUE walls → GoToColor(blue) → arrive on BLACK floor → Stop
- Escape clause: if on BLACK but near GREEN walls, move away
- The "see blue" transition (C=7, l=3) is the key signal

I'll keep the best-looking topologies from Round 2 and add variations:

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
Since I'm not receiving actual score feedback, I need to reason more carefully about what should work best for this mission.

**Deep re-analysis:**
- Both BLACK strips (target and non-target) have black floors. Floor color alone can't distinguish them.
- The ONLY way to distinguish target (BLUE walls) from wrong strip (GREEN walls) is via camera perception of wall colors.
- Key insight: GoToColor(blue=3) will steer toward BLUE walls continuously. Once in the blue strip, the robot is on BLACK floor AND sees blue walls. GoAwayColor(green=2) pushes away from green walls.
- The most reliable strategy: always GoToColor(blue), stop when on BLACK floor (since arriving on black near blue walls is the goal). But robots in the GREEN strip also see black floor - they need to escape via GoAwayColor(green) or GoToColor(blue).
- Critical fix: when on BLACK floor but seeing GREEN walls → move away from green / toward blue.

**Best topology:** GoToColor(blue) → on BLACK floor → Stop → if sees GREEN walls, go back to GoToColor(blue) OR GoAwayColor(green).

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
Since I'm still not receiving score feedback, I need to think more carefully about what's likely working and what isn't.

**Critical re-analysis:**

The mission says "AGGREGATION" but the scoring rule is about robots being in the target region (BLUE-walled strip). This is actually a **homing/foraging** task, not classic aggregation.

Key perception issues:
- Both strips have BLACK floors - can't use floor color alone to identify target
- Must use BLUE wall color (clr=3) to identify the target strip
- GoToColor(blue=3) moves toward blue walls = correct direction
- When robot is IN the blue strip and close to the blue wall, it may stop seeing blue (wall behind it) - need to handle this
- GoAwayColor(green=2) from center pushes toward blue strip from the green side

**What likely works best:**
- Simple 2-state: GoToColor(blue) → Stop on BLACK floor. The robot arrives at blue wall, floor is black, stops. Risk: stops in green strip too.
- Better: Stop only when on BLACK AND not seeing GREEN walls
- The transition "on black floor + not seeing green" is hard to express directly
- Alternative: Use probability on black floor transition to be selective, keep GoToColor(blue) active most of the time

**Best guess at winning design:** GoToColor(blue) as primary, transition to Stop on BLACK with high probability, escape Stop if seeing GREEN walls.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
Since I'm receiving no score feedback after 5 rounds, I need to reason purely from first principles and be more systematic about what should work.

**Fundamental re-think:**

The core problem: robots start in the center (GRAY floor) and must reach the BLUE-walled strip (BLACK floor). 

The **simplest possible correct solution**: GoToColor(blue=3) forever. This always moves toward blue walls. Once a robot is pressed against the blue wall, it stays there. No stopping needed - the wall blocks it. This might actually be optimal!

**Why stopping on BLACK might be wrong**: A robot stopping on black floor might be in the GREEN strip. GoToColor(blue) while being blocked by a blue wall is essentially "stopped at the target" naturally.

**Alternative insight**: Maybe the issue is that GoToColor loses sight of blue walls when very close. Then exploration/spiral helps re-acquire them.

**New approach - single state GoToColor(blue) with small random escape:**

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
Since I've received no score feedback across 6 rounds, I need to fundamentally reconsider my approach.

**Deep re-examination:**

The mission is called "AGGREGATION" but scores based on robots being outside the BLUE-walled strip. The scoring says "score increases by 1 for every robot outside the target region" - so this is really a **homing** task.

**What I may have gotten wrong:**
- I've been using `--nstates 1` only once. Let me try it prominently.
- The single-state GoToColor(blue) is the purest solution: always move toward blue walls. The robot naturally presses against the blue wall and stays. No need for stop states.
- But: robots starting far from blue walls in gray zone will take time. GoToColor needs to see blue to work - if no blue visible, robot may spin randomly.
- When no color is visible, GoToColor behaves like exploration. So it may self-correct.

**Key new hypothesis:** The 1-state GoToColor(blue) might be optimal. I've been overcomplicating this with multi-state machines. Let me heavily sample around this plus simple 2-state variants.

**Also trying:** GoAwayColor(green) as primary - pushes robots away from green walls toward blue side naturally.

FSM: --nstates 1 --s0 8 --vel0 1.0 --clr0 3 --cle0 0 --n0 0

FSM: --nstates 2 --s0 8 --vel0 1.0 --clr0 3 --cle0 0 --n0 1 --n0x0 1 --c0x0 0 --p0x0 0.95 --s1 1 --cle1 0 --n1 1 --n1x0 0 --c1x0 1 --p1x0 0.9

FSM: --nstates 2 --s0 8 --vel0 1.0 --clr0 3 --cle0 0 --n0 1 --n0x0 1 --c0x0 0 --p0x0 0.98 --s1 1 --cle1 0 --n1 1 --n1x0 0 --c1x0 1 --p1x0 0.95

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
Since I still receive no score data, I must reason from scratch about what actually works in ARGoS for this specific setup.

**Critical insight I may have missed:**

The problem says "AGGREGATION mission" - but the score is about robots being in the target region. In AutoMoDe's standard AGGREGATION mission, the score might actually be counting robots NOT aggregated together, not robots outside a region. Let me re-read...

"At every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region. The target region is the strip whose bordering walls are BLUE."

So it IS a homing task. My GoToColor(blue=3) approach should be directionally correct.

**Why might it be failing?**
- Color IDs: maybe BLUE walls = color 6 (cyan) not 3? The problem says "BLUE walls" and color list has 3=blue, 6=cyan. Let me try BOTH.
- Maybe green walls = color 1 (red)? No, 2=green seems right.
- The camera perceives colored objects - walls ARE colored objects the camera can see.

**New hypothesis: try clr=6 (cyan) for blue walls, and clr=1 (red) might be wrong green.**

Let me systematically try different color ID combinations for blue walls (3 and 6) and green walls (1 and 2).

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
Since I still have no score feedback after 8 rounds, I need to think very carefully about what could be going wrong.

**Fundamental re-examination of color IDs:**

The problem states colors are {1,2,3,4,5,6}. In ARGoS/AutoMoDe the standard color mapping for e-puck LEDs and object colors is typically:
- 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan

But WALL colors in the arena description say "BLUE walls" and "GREEN walls". The camera condition (C=7) detects colored objects. Walls ARE colored objects.

However - I just realized: **the LED color parameter `--cle` uses {0,4,5,6} not {0,1,2,3,4,5,6}**. This suggests the color numbering for objects perceived by camera may differ from LED colors.

**More critically:** Maybe the walls aren't detectable by the robot camera at all, or the color IDs for walls differ. Let me try a completely different approach: **use floor color only**.

Both strips are BLACK. Gray = middle. Strategy:
- If on GRAY → explore/move forward to find black
- If on BLACK → stop (you're in a strip, 50% chance it's the right one... but can't distinguish!)

Wait - if we can't distinguish strips by floor color alone, and wall colors may not be reliably perceived, maybe the best strategy exploits **symmetry breaking via neighbor attraction**: aggregate in one strip using social cues.

**New pure floor-color approach:** Explore until BLACK floor, then stop. Expected score = roughly half robots in wrong strip × 1200 steps. Not great.

**Back to basics - try ALL color IDs for GoToColor systematically, plus neighbor-based aggregation:**

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
After 9 rounds with no score feedback, I need to make my final push with maximum diversity and coverage of all plausible approaches.

**Final reasoning:**

I've tried: clr=3(blue), clr=6(cyan), clr=1(red), clr=2(green), clr=4(yellow), clr=5(magenta) for GoToColor. I've tried 1-state, 2-state, 3-state, 4-state machines. Without feedback I can't know what worked.

**What I haven't tried enough:**
- Pure floor-based navigation: explore→stop on BLACK. Simple but might work if robots can't perceive walls well.
- Attraction/FlockCenter to aggregate robots already in the strip
- The possibility that the camera simply doesn't detect walls at all in this arena setup, making all color-based transitions useless
- Using neighbor count conditions to detect being in a cluster (robots accumulate in the target strip)

**Final strategy - cover all bases:**
1. Keep best candidates from each color family
2. Add pure floor-based machines  
3. Add neighbor/social machines
4. Add mixed approaches

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

At every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region. The score does not depend on how close the robots are to each other.

The arena has two strips at opposite ends. The floor is BLACK in both strips and GRAY in between. The target region is the strip whose bordering walls are BLUE; the walls bordering the other strip are GREEN.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 20 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.50 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
